# MedEvo: Medical Reasoning Evolution through Knowledge Navigation

Xiaolian<sub>g</sub> Wan<sub>g</sub><sup>1,2,\*</sup>, Zhuo Chen<sup>2,\*</sup>, Chuan<sub>y</sub>an<sub>g</sub> Zhen<sub>g</sub><sup>2</sup>, Quanbin Wan<sub>g</sub><sup>2</sup>, Jian<sub>g</sub>wei Lao<sup>2</sup>, Yin<sub>g</sub>ao Liu<sup>2</sup> Jiajia Liu<sup>2</sup>, Jinmin<sub>g</sub> Cao<sup>2</sup>, Hon<sub>g</sub>lin Lin<sup>3,4</sup>, Zhihon<sub>g</sub> Lu<sup>2</sup>, Hao Jin<sup>1</sup>, Jian Wan<sub>g</sub><sup>2</sup>, Lĳun Wu<sup>2,3,†</sup>

<sup>1</sup>Z<sup>h</sup>ejiang University <sup>2A</sup>nt Group

<sup>3</sup>Shan<sub>g</sub>hai Artificial Intelli<sub>g</sub>ence Laborator<sub>y</sub> <sup>4</sup>Shan<sub>g</sub>hai Jiao Ton<sub>g</sub> Universit<sub>y</sub>

## Abstract

Medical multimodal reasoning models are typically trained on teacher-generated rationales, discarding the va<sup>l</sup>i<sup>d</sup> reasonin in stu<sup>d</sup>ents<sup>’</sup> own <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> trajectories, w<sup>h</sup>i<sup>l</sup>e enera<sup>l</sup>-<sup>d</sup>omain correction met<sup>h</sup>o<sup>d</sup>s o<sup>f</sup>er <sup>l</sup>itt<sup>l</sup>e ui<sup>d</sup>ance for errors in ima e readin , anatomical context, or diferential dia nosis. We ro ose MedEvo, which uses medical knowledge, namely a tag taxonomy and error-type diagnosis, to navigate both trajectory repair and data selection. For each failed rollout, a gold-blind auditor locates the first consequential error and provides a minimal, answer-free hint; the same student retains the valid prefix and regenerates the erroneous sufix. After each round, MedEvo identifies capability gaps across medical tags on a held-out feedback set and prioritizes related questions for new rollouts and repairs. With supervised fine-tuning (SFT) alone, MedEvo raises Qwen3.5-9B mean accurac across seven benchmarks from 65.57% to 69.12% (47.40% to 54.50% on the multimodal track of MedX<sub>p</sub>ertQA (MedX<sub>p</sub>ertQA-MM)), sur<sub>p</sub>assin<sub>g</sub> teacher-distillation SFT (66.93%). It also raises Qwen3.5-35B and Qwen3.5-397B mean accuracy from 67.91% to 73.04% and from 71.83% to 74.95%, respectively. Our analyses show that failed trajectories contain reasoning worth preserving, that repair success does not imply learning value, and that independent feedback is key to sustained improvement.

Project: https://pork-pay.github.io/MedEvo/

GitHub: https://github.com/pork-pay/MedEvo

## 2 Related Work

## 2.1 Medical Multimodal Reasoning and Supervision Quality

Medical vision-lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models use biomedical instruction tunin<sub>g</sub> [2, 3], <sub>g</sub>eneralist trainin<sub>g</sub> in Lin<sub>g</sub>shu [11], and bilin<sub>g</sub>ual BiMediX2 trainin<sub>g</sub> [12]. MedGemma builds medical vision-lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models on Gemma 3 [13]. Structured knowled<sub>g</sub>e can also be inte<sub>g</sub>rated with multimodal learnin<sub>g</sub> throu<sub>g</sub>h knowled<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs [14]. Be<sub>y</sub>ond answer accuracy, process-level audits separate visual recognition, knowledge application, and evidence integration [15, 16], and rubric-based evaluations can miss hallucinations, motivating factuality checks [17].

Supervision quality depends on reasoning content and student compatibility: process supervision provides step level feedback [18], on-<sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> distillation studies model com<sub>p</sub>atibilit<sub>y</sub> [9], and ChainPrune [19] reduces redundant reasonin<sub>g</sub> while distin<sub>g</sub>uishin<sub>g</sub> structural eficienc<sub>y</sub> from token len<sub>g</sub>th. JOLT makes student com<sub>p</sub>atibilit<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>licit: even a <sup>h</sup>ig<sup>h</sup>er-per<sup>f</sup>orming privi<sup>l</sup>ege<sup>d</sup> teac<sup>h</sup>er can in<sup>d</sup>uce an un<sup>h</sup>e<sup>l</sup>p<sup>f</sup>u<sup>l</sup> stu<sup>d</sup>ent up<sup>d</sup>ate, motivating joint teac<sup>h</sup>er–stu<sup>d</sup>ent trainin<sub>g</sub> with re<sub>g</sub>ularization toward the student’s current <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> [20]. MedEvo dia<sub>g</sub>noses failures with a medical <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>b<sub>oo</sub>k<sub>, screen</sub>i<sub>ng repa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> f<sub>or answer correc</sub>t<sub>ness, va</sub>lid b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>egr</sub>it<sub>y, an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>-re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>earna</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub>

## 2.2 Learning from Failures and Localized Repair

Failed trajectories can su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> su<sub>p</sub>ervision: Learnin<sub>g</sub> from Mistakes (LEMA) trains on mistake–correction <sub>p</sub>airs [6], TrajFusion combines incorrect and correct trajectories [10], and HHD <sub>g</sub>uides recover<sub>y</sub> with concise hints [1]. Identif<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> an error can enable its correction [5], and backtrackin<sub>g</sub> distillation and selective re<sub>g</sub>eneration stud<sub>y</sub> where correction should begin [21, 22]. ## 2.3 Data Evolution and Recursive Self-Improvement

Self-Tau<sub>g</sub>ht Reasoner (STaR) and Reinforced Self-Trainin<sub>g</sub> with Ex<sub>p</sub>ectation–Maximization (ReST<sup>EM</sup>) build su<sub>p</sub>ervision b<sub>y</sub> filterin<sub>g</sub> self-<sub>g</sub>enerated reasonin<sub>g</sub> for correctness [7, 8], and micro-reflective self-distillation adds ca<sub>p</sub>abilit<sub>y</sub>-de<sub>p</sub>endent trajector<sub>y</sub> selection [24]. MediSkill-Evo [25] instead evolves validated clinical knowled<sub>g</sub>e banks <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one.</sub> W<sub>or</sub>k <sub>on</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> <sub>me</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>ves</sub> <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ures,</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erences, c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>ki<sub>ng can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es on</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>new an</sub>d <sub>prev</sub>i<sub>ous</sub>l<sub>y seen cases w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> l<sub>eav</sub>i<sub>ng mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>p</sub>arameters unchan<sub>g</sub>ed [26]. ## 3 Method

Knowledge Navigation. We define Knowledge Navigation as a feedback-guided process that uses structured domain knowled e and failure dia nosis to choose where to intervene, how to recover, and what to learn next. ## 3.2.3 Prefix Preservation and Same-Student Continuation

Like LEMA [6], we train on corrected mistakes, but the correction is <sub>g</sub>enerated b<sub>y</sub> the student itself. The boundar<sub>y</sub> splits the failed trajectory into a preserved prefix and an erroneous sufix,

$$
\begin{array} { c c } { { r ^ { - } = p _ { b } \oplus s ^ { - } , } } & { { ~ p _ { b } = ( s _ { 1 } , \ldots , s _ { b } ) . } } \end{array}\tag{8}
$$

H<sub>ere</sub> $b \in \{ 0 , \ldots , K - 1 \} , p _ { 0 }$ is empty, and ⊕ denotes token-sequence concatenation. We keep $p _ { b }$ verbatim, append th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>n</sub>t <sub>ren</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l<sub>-</sub>l<sub>anguage</sub> <sub>recovery</sub> <sub>cue</sub> $g ( h )$ <sub>, an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t th<sub>e same c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nue:</sub>

$$
s ^ { + } \sim \pi _ { \theta _ { t } } \bigl ( \cdot \mid x , p _ { b } \oplus g ( h ) \bigr ) , \qquad r ^ { * } = p _ { b } \oplus g ( h ) \oplus s ^ { + } .\tag{9}
$$

Th<sub>e ques</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ays</sub> in th<sub>e use</sub>r m<sub>essage,</sub> $p _ { b } \oplus g ( h )$ <sub>pre</sub>fill<sub>s an un</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t <sub>message, an</sub>d th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d <sub>au</sub>dit is stored only as metadata. With the prefix fixed, we sample up to five sufixes per failed trajectory, stopping at the first admitted continuation, and send answer-correct continuations to admission (§ 3.2.4; Appendix D.4).

## 3.2.4 Repair Admission and Training Targets

A continuation is admitted only if it passes answer, boundary, integrity, and learnability checks. Learnability is scored on the new sufix alone, so that a valid prefix cannot mask the dificulty of the correction. Adapting the instruction-followin<sub>g</sub> dificult<sub>y</sub> of Li et al. [27], we measure how much the sufix de<sub>p</sub>ends on the task in<sub>p</sub>ut:

$$
D _ { \mathrm { r e p a i r } } = { \frac { { \mathcal { L } } _ { t } ( s ^ { + } \mid x , p _ { b } ) } { { \mathcal { L } } _ { t } ( s ^ { + } \mid p _ { b } ) } } ,\tag{10}
$$

## 4 Experiments

## 4.1 Experimental Setup

Models and data. Our main backbone is Qwen3.5-9B [28]; Qwen3.5-35B and Qwen3.5-397B test scalabilit<sub>y</sub> (§ 5.3). The t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>poo</sub>l <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> 81<sub>,</sub>293 <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit<sub>s</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>pu</sub>bli<sub>c</sub> m<sub>e</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>l d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>spa</sub>nnin<sub>g</sub> nin<sub>e</sub> m<sub>o</sub>d<sub>a</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> cate<sub>g</sub>ories and five dificult<sub>y</sub> levels (Fi<sub>g</sub>. 3), and is dedu<sub>p</sub>licated b<sub>y</sub> <sub>q</sub>uestion and source identifiers<sub>,</sub> <sub>p</sub>atient/case identifiers <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e,</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> h<sub>as</sub>h<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>near-</sub>d<sub>up</sub>li<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> (A<sub>pp</sub>endix C.1.4). ## 4.2 Main Results

Tab. 2 reports the results. On Qwen3.5-9B, MedEvo raises mean accuracy from 65.57% to 69.12% and improves all seven benchmarks, with the largest gain on MedXpertQA-MM [4] (47.40% → 54.50%). It outperforms HHD [1], TrajFusion [10], and teacher-distillation SFT, whose mean accuracies are 65.16%, 66.45%, and 66.93%, res<sub>p</sub>ectivel . On Qwen3.5-35B, mean accurac<sub>y</sub> rises from 67.91% to 73.04% (§ 5.3). A <sub>q</sub>uestion-level <sub>p</sub>aired bootstra<sub>p</sub> (10,000 resam les; Tab. 3, ri ht) ields 95% confidence intervals (CIs) that exclude zero on ever benchmark. ## 4.3 Accuracy Gains and Repair Workload

Mean accurac increases from 65.57% to 67.22%, 67.42%, and 69.12% across rounds (Tab. 3, left), while the number of <sub>q</sub>uestions sent for re<sub>p</sub>air falls from 27,233 to 23,620 and re<sub>p</sub>air success remains near 71% (Tab. 1). Thus, the later rounds im<sub>p</sub>rove benchmark <sub>p</sub>erformance with a smaller <sub>q</sub>uestion-level re<sub>p</sub>air workload; § 5.2 isolates the role of feedback-<sub>g</sub>uided selection. Individual benchmarks still fluctuate, as illustrated b<sub>y</sub> PathVQA [33]. Modalit<sub>y</sub> and error-type statistics on the fixed feedback set guide subsequent selection (Appendix B.3); the seven held-out benchmarks assess generalization.

## 5 Ablation Studies and Analysis

We analyze repair, feedback, scale, and learning value, using Qwen3.5-9B except in scaling experiments [28].

## 5.4 Learning Value of Repair Supervision

Beyond repair success, we examine three questions about what makes repair supervision useful (Fig. 5).

Which trajectories are worth repairing? We compare the 8,000 questions with the longest failed trajectories against 8,000 random <sub>q</sub>uestions, each used in one round. The lon<sub>g</sub>est set has lower re<sub>p</sub>air success (55.70% vs. 67.50%) but yields higher downstream accuracy (66.78% vs. 64.35%; Fig. 5a), favoring learning-based trajectory selection.

How many hints help? All main experiments provide one hint per failed trajectory. Using the longest-8k subset <sup>f</sup>rom t<sup>h</sup>e previous comparison, we vary t<sup>h</sup>e num<sup>b</sup>er o<sup>f h</sup>ints per <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> trajectory <sup>f</sup>rom 1 to 3, 5, an<sup>d</sup> 8, <sup>k</sup>eeping <sup>fi</sup>ve <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>emp</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> M<sub>ean</sub> <sub>accuracy</sub> i<sub>s</sub> 66<sub>.</sub>78% <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>one</sub> hi<sub>n</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d 67<sub>.</sub>01% <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>ree,</sub> th<sub>en</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> 66<sub>.</sub>74% <sub>an</sub>d 64.10% with five and ei<sub>g</sub>ht (Fi<sub>g</sub>. 5b). ## 7 Conclusion and Discussion

We intro<sup>d</sup>uce<sup>d</sup> Me<sup>d</sup>Evo to stu<sup>d</sup>y <sup>h</sup>ow <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> me<sup>d</sup>ica<sup>l</sup> reasoning trajectories can <sup>b</sup>ecome use<sup>f</sup>u<sup>l</sup> supervision. Knowledge Navigation links local repair to subsequent data selection: a gold-blind auditor provides a minimal, <sub>answer-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> hi<sub>n</sub>t<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>preserves</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>regenera</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>erroneous</sub> <sub>su</sub>fi<sub>x,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d feedback set directs later repairs toward remaining weaknesses. With SFT alone, MedEvo improves Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3.5-35B, and Qwen3.5-397B across seven benchmarks, with consistent <sub>g</sub>ains across the three Qwen3.5-9B runs. The central lesson from these experiments is that correcting a response and constructing useful supervision are diferent objectives. Preserving valid reasoning, selecting repairs for learning value, and using independent f<sub>ee</sub>db<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t l<sub>earns.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> b<sub>e assesse</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>rnin<sub>g,</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>o</sub>n<sub>gs</sub>id<sub>e</sub> r<sub>epa</sub>ir <sub>success</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>e</sub>n<sub>ce</sub> <sub>g</sub>r<sub>ou</sub>ndin<sub>g.</sub>

## 7.1 Insights for Medical Self-Evolution

T<sup>h</sup>e practica<sup>l</sup> question is <sup>h</sup>ow to a<sup>ll</sup>ocate repair e<sup>f</sup>ort: w<sup>h</sup>ic<sup>h</sup> parts o<sup>f</sup> a <sup>f</sup>ai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> trajectory remain use<sup>f</sup>u<sup>l</sup>, w<sup>h</sup>ic<sup>h</sup> <sub>co</sub>rr<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld b<sub>eco</sub>m<sub>e</sub> tr<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g</sub> t<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd whi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>capa</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>gaps</sub> w<sub>a</sub>rr<sub>a</sub>nt n<sub>e</sub>w d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a.</sub>

From diagnosis to intervention. Medical tags describe the clinical context, while error types identify a recovery operation <sup>f</sup>or t<sup>h</sup>e current trajectory. T<sup>h</sup>e two gui<sup>d</sup>e <sup>d</sup>i<sup>f</sup>erent <sup>d</sup>ecisions: tag-<sup>l</sup>eve<sup>l f</sup>ee<sup>db</sup>ac<sup>k</sup> prioritizes questions, and local error dia<sub>g</sub>nosis <sub>g</sub>uides the hint and rollback boundar<sub>y</sub>. The re<sub>p</sub>air and feedback ablations (Tabs. 4 and 5) <sub>suppor</sub>t thi<sub>s</sub> di<sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ro</sub>l<sub>es</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> M<sub>e</sub>dE<sub>vo.</sub>

Evolution through supervision. The taxonomy and error codebook remain fixed in the present implementation; selection priorities and accepted supervision change with the student. Each round retrains from $M _ { 0 }$ <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>so</sub> inf<sub>o</sub>rm<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>passes</sub> <sub>ac</sub>r<sub>oss</sub> r<sub>ou</sub>nd<sub>s</sub> thr<sub>oug</sub>h <sub>supe</sub>r<sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n<sub>.</sub> R<sub>eg</sub>r<sub>ess</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd <sub>pe</sub>r<sub>s</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>e</sub>nt w<sub>ea</sub>kn<sub>esses</sub> th<sub>en</sub> <sub>re</sub>di<sub>rec</sub>t <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>t <sub>repa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>or</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Failed trajectories retain valuable reasoning. Sufix continuation outperforms full rewriting in both repair success and downstream accuracy (Tab. 4), supporting the value of preserving the valid prefix. In the trajectory-selection ex<sub>p</sub>eriment, the lon<sub>g</sub>est-8k subset has lower re<sub>p</sub>air success than random selection (55.70% vs. 67.50%) but <sub>y</sub>ields hi<sub>g</sub>her downstream accurac<sub>y</sub> (66.78% vs. 64.35%; Fi<sub>g</sub>. 5a). Re<sub>p</sub>air success alone would therefore rank these two <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ces</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng ou</sub>t<sub>comes.</sub>

Repairability and learning value are diferent objectives. The com arison above shows a diver ence between re air success an<sup>d l</sup>earning va<sup>l</sup>ue in t<sup>h</sup>e teste<sup>d</sup> su<sup>b</sup>sets; it <sup>d</sup>oes not iso<sup>l</sup>ate trajectory <sup>l</sup>engt<sup>h f</sup>rom question <sup>d</sup>i<sup>fi</sup>cu<sup>l</sup>ty. In t<sup>h</sup>e hint<sub>-cou</sub>nt <sub>e</sub>x<sub>pe</sub>rim<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>,</sub> thr<sub>ee</sub> hint<sub>s y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t <sub>o</sub>b<sub>se</sub>r<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>accu</sub>r<sub>acy, o</sub>nl<sub>y</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>23 <sub>pe</sub>r<sub>ce</sub>nt<sub>age po</sub>int<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>ove o</sub>n<sub>e</sub> hint<sub>,</sub> while five and eight hints perform worse (Fig. 5b). Guidance should be calibrated against subsequent learning, <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> i<sub>ncrease</sub>d <sub>so</sub>l<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o ease correc</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> L<sub>oca</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> h<sub>as a re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> th<sub>e erroneous-s</sub>t<sub>ep ra</sub>t<sub>e r</sub>i<sub>ses</sub> from 16.2% to 64.1% across entro<sub>py</sub> bins (Fi<sub>g</sub>. 6c), makin<sub>g</sub> entro<sub>py</sub> a useful ins<sub>p</sub>ection cue in this anal<sub>y</sub>sis, while the <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence-groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>au</sub>dit d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>nes w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er an error</sub> i<sub>s presen</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere regenera</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld b<sub>eg</sub>i<sub>n.</sub>

The source of feedback matters. Training-error feedback improves fit to the training data while benchmark accuracy declines; fixed-set feedback improves benchmark accuracy across rounds (Fig. 5c,d). In these experiments, training failures supply repair candidates, while independent feedback helps prioritize capability gaps. The distinction <sub>p</sub>r<sub>eve</sub>nt<sub>s p</sub>r<sub>og</sub>r<sub>ess o</sub>n th<sub>e</sub> tr<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g e</sub>x<sub>a</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>es</sub> fr<sub>o</sub>m <sub>se</sub>r<sub>v</sub>in<sub>g as</sub> th<sub>e so</sub>l<sub>e c</sub>rit<sub>e</sub>ri<sub>o</sub>n f<sub>o</sub>r <sub>a</sub>ll<sub>oca</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>r r<sub>epa</sub>ir <sub>e</sub>f<sub>o</sub>rt<sub>.</sub>

Answer correctness and grounding are complementary. The human audit assesses answer correctness and visual grounding separately (§ 6.1). Its results motivate checking evidence use alongside accuracy and hint non-leakage w<sup>h</sup>en a<sup>d</sup>mitting repaire<sup>d</sup> trajectories as supervision.

## 7.2 Limitations and Future Directions

The current implementation has a fixed knowledge structure. Quota allocation. ## B.2 Round-wise Data Construction

The final training set remains fixed at 81,293 questions in each round, combining qualified original correct trajectories with accepted repairs and retained valid historical targets. Leafspecificity and multi-label assignment. CT branches into noncontrast, contrast-enhanced, CT angiography (CTA), hi<sub>g</sub>h-resolution CT (HRCT), and low-dose CT; MRI includes T1- and T2-wei<sub>g</sub>hted ima<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub>, fluid-attenuated inversion recover<sub>y</sub> (FLAIR), difusion-wei<sub>g</sub>hted ima<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> (DWI), ma<sub>g</sub>netic resonance an<sub>g</sub>io<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub> (MRA), functional MRI (fMRI), ma<sub>g</sub>netic resonance s<sub>p</sub>ectrosco<sub>py</sub> (MRS), and susce<sub>p</sub>tibilit<sub>y</sub>-wei<sub>g</sub>hted ima<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> (SWI). Anatom<sub>y</sub> can descend from thorax to lun<sub>g</sub>/airwa<sub>y</sub> and then to a lun<sub>g</sub> lobe<sub>,</sub> or from abdomen to <sub>p</sub>ancreas and then head/bod<sub>y</sub>/tail. An artifact, normal structure, or unsupported feature is</td><td>Reinspect the relevant region.</td></tr><tr><td>V2 Hallucinated or misidentified finding</td><td>read as a lesion.</td><td>Check direct visual support.</td></tr><tr><td>V3 Anatomy or laterality error</td><td>The organ, level, compartment, or side conflicts with</td><td>Recheck landmarks and</td></tr><tr><td>V4 Modality, sequence, or</td><td>visible evidence. A lesion is matched incorrectly across images, slices, or</td><td>before reading features. Align images and anatomical</td></tr><tr><td>correspondence error</td><td>views.</td><td>locations.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3">R: Reasoning error.</td></tr><tr><td>Code / subtype</td><td>Diagnostic criterion</td><td>Navigation operation</td></tr><tr><td>R1 Omitted evidence</td><td>An observed finding or stated fact is omitted from inference.</td><td>Reintroduce the omitted evidence.</td></tr><tr><td>R2 Conflicting evidence integration</td><td>Correctly extracted evidence conflicts and is not reconciled.</td><td>Reconcile supporting and opposing evidence.</td></tr><tr><td>R3 Unsupported inference or causal leap</td><td>A conclusion lacks the required logical or causal link.</td><td>Restore a testable intermediate inference.</td></tr><tr><td>R4 Temporal or quantitative error</td><td>Correctly read facts are combined with an incorrect temporal or numerical relation.</td><td>Check chronology, units, or calculation.</td></tr><tr><td>R5 Task constraint or answer-mapping error</td><td>The reasoning violates the requested target, negation, or option mapping.</td><td>Recheck the requested target and answer mapping.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3">D: Wrong differential.</td></tr><tr><td>Code / subtype</td><td>Diagnostic criterion</td><td>Navigation operation</td></tr><tr><td>D1 Omitted candidate</td><td>A necessary evidence-compatible alternative is not considered.</td><td>Broaden the candidate comparison.</td></tr><tr><td>D2 Missing discriminative comparison</td><td>Alternatives are listed without comparing discriminative evidence.</td><td>Identify discriminative evidence.</td></tr><tr><td>D3 Premature closure or anchoring</td><td>An initial diagnosis is fixed before counterevidence is considered.</td><td>Revisit counterevidence and alternatives.</td></tr><tr><td>D4 Incorrect candidate ranking</td><td>Known support, counterevidence, or priors are weighted incorrectly.</td><td>Compare the strength of evidence.</td></tr><tr><td>D5 Misapplied exclusion</td><td>An alternative is excluded using insufficient negative evidence or a nonessential criterion.</td><td>Check whether exclusion is justified.</td></tr></table>

K: Knowledge gap.
<table><tr><td>Code / subtype</td><td>Diagnostic criterion</td><td>Navigation operation</td></tr><tr><td>K1 Missing finding interpretation</td><td>The required medical meaning of a finding or term is missing.</td><td>Cue the relevant conceptual relation.</td></tr><tr><td>K2 Missing diagnostic criterion</td><td>A required diagnostic, grading, or staging rule is unavailable.</td><td>Cue the relevant rule category.</td></tr><tr><td>K3 Missing anatomy or pathophysiology</td><td>Knowledge linking anatomy, mechanism, and</td><td>Bridge the minimal</td></tr><tr><td>K4 Missing applicability</td><td>manifestations is missing. A general rule is known but its prerequisites or exceptions</td><td>mechanistic relation. Cue the applicability check.</td></tr><tr><td>condition or exception K5 Missing investigation or management knowledge</td><td>are missing. A next-investigation or management task requires an unavailable principle.</td><td>Cue the decision principle</td></tr></table>

Trainin<sub>g</sub> uses brain floatin<sub>g</sub>-<sub>p</sub>oint 16 (BF16) <sub>p</sub>recision on <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hics <sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub> units (GPUs). <table><tr><td>Prompt D.3: Stage-1 Entropy-Guided Medical Trajectory Audit</td></tr><tr><td>You are a senior medical reviewer auditing a failed multimodal reasoning trajectory segmented into semantic steps  $s _ { 1 } , \ldots , s _ { K } .$ </td></tr><tr><td>You are given:</td></tr><tr><td>(1) the medical image(s); (2) the clinical question and answer options;</td></tr><tr><td>(3) the failed student trajectory segmented into semantic steps;</td></tr><tr><td>(4) STEP_ENTROPY: the uncertainty score  $E _ { k } ^ { \ast }$  of every step;</td></tr><tr><td>(5) NORMALIZED_ENTROPY: the normalized uncertainty score of every step;</td></tr><tr><td>(6) LOCAL_SURGE: the local increase  $\Delta E _ { k }$  relative to the preceding step history; </td></tr><tr><td>(7) ENTROPY_ANCHOR: the step proposed by the uncertainty locator;</td></tr><tr><td>(8) ENTROPY_WINDOW: the compact candidate window surrounding that anchor, and</td></tr><tr><td>ENTROPY_THRESHOLD if a threshold was used. (9) OBSERVABLE_TAGS: per-image modality and other context supported by the original input, with evidence</td></tr><tr><td>provenance; never hidden answer-derived tags. The entropy signal guides audit localization. High uncertainty can reflect a genuine failure, but it can also arise</td></tr><tr><td>from difficult terminology, ambiguous visual evidence, or a medically valid differential diagnosis. Combine the entropy proposal with the image, clinical evidence, and dependencies between reasoning steps to identify the</td></tr><tr><td>first consequential failure. Error-conditioned hint construction. Construct the hint from OBSERVABLE_TAGS, ERROR_TYPE,</td></tr><tr><td>ERROR_SUBTYPE, and KEY_ERROR. Select a recovery operation addressing the diagnosed failure:</td></tr><tr><td>• Visual misreading: direct attention to the relevant region, visual attribute, or cross-image correspondence.</td></tr><tr><td>• Reasoning error: revisit an unsupported inference or reconnect observed evidence to the requested decision. • Differential diagnosis error: compare discriminative evidence or reconsider an underexamined alternative.</td></tr><tr><td>• Knowledge gap: identify the relevant concept, rule, or applicability condition to reconsider.</td></tr><tr><td>Specify one concrete next operation grounded in the current question and trajectory. Express it as a brief</td></tr><tr><td>recovery cue without supplying the corrected finding, final diagnosis, or answer option. Audit procedure:</td></tr><tr><td>(1) Inspect ENTROPY_WINDOW first. Determine whether its local entropy peak or surge corresponds to an unsupported observation, invalid inference, incorrect differential comparison, or missing medical principle.</td></tr><tr><td>(2) Trace evidence dependencies backward from ENTROPY_ANCHOR. Decide whether the decisive failure begins inside the proposed window or an earlier step introduced the unsupported premise that caused the later</td></tr><tr><td>entropy increase. (3) Do not automatically select the highest-entropy step. Justify the final AUDIT_ANCHOR and</td></tr><tr><td>SAFE_PREFIX_END using the medical image, clinical information, and logical dependencies.</td></tr><tr><td>(4) Prefer a compact localized repair and preserve every preceding step that remains medically and logically valid.</td></tr><tr><td>(5) You may move the audit anchor outside ENTROPY_WINDOW when the proposal misses the first consequential failure, but briefly explain why the entropy signal was misleading.</td></tr><tr><td>Return a JavaScript Object Notation (JSON) object with the following fields, grounded strictly in the provided</td></tr><tr><td>image and text: (a) ACTION: REPAIR or SKIP.</td></tr><tr><td>(b) STEP_SUMMARY: a brief one-sentence semantic summary for each step.</td></tr><tr><td>(c) ENTROPY_ASSESSMENT, containing: proposed_anchor, proposed_window, proposal_status, and</td></tr><tr><td>brief_reason. proposal_status must be one of {HIT, PARTIAL_HIT, MISS, NON_ERROR_UNCERTAINTY}. HIT</td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td>means that the first consequential failure is at or immediately adjacent to the proposed anchor; PARTIAL_HIT</td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td>means that the proposal covers the propagated error region but the unsupported premise begins earlier; MISS</td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td></td></tr><tr><td>means that the first failure lies outside the proposed window; (e) SAFE_PREFIX_END, containing step_index and the exact verbatim_ending_span of the last fully valid step before the repair region. (i) MINIMAL_HINT: a brief recovery cue with flexible wording, conditioned on observable medical tags and the</td></tr><tr><td>diagnosed local error. Name the evidence or reasoning operation to revisit. Do not include the error-category label or audit explanation. (1) DIFFERENTIAL_FOCUS: the discriminative axis to compare, or null.</td></tr><tr><td>(m) STOP_CONDITION: the evidence condition under which the continuation should commit to an answer and</td></tr><tr><td>stop.</td></tr><tr><td>Constraints: - Do not access hidden disease labels or hidden lesion coordinates. - Do not state a gold answer, corrected diagnosis, or correct option letter.</td></tr></table>

## F.1 Medical Reasoning, Distillation, and Data Selection

Medical vision-language models use biomedical instruction tuning [2, 3], generalist reasoning with Lingshu [11], and English–Arabic medical image understanding in BiMediX2 [12]. Verifier-guided search, knowledge-enhanced s<sub>y</sub>nthesis, and reinforcement learnin<sub>g</sub>[40, 41, 42], to<sub>g</sub>ether with <sub>p</sub>arallel reasonin<sub>g</sub> and multi-a<sub>g</sub>ent collaboration[43, 44, 45], im<sub>p</sub>rove reasonin<sub>g</sub> ca<sub>p</sub>abilities. Process-level dia<sub>g</sub>nostics and distillation audits distin<sub>g</sub>uish answer correctness from reliable visual reco<sub>g</sub>nition, knowled<sub>g</sub>e a<sub>pp</sub>lication, and evidence inte<sub>g</sub>ration[15, 16, 46].

MMVistaReason [47] combines o<sub>p</sub>en-data curation with ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub>-aware <sub>p</sub>ost-trainin<sub>g</sub> for multimodal reasonin<sub>g</sub>.

Medical hallucinations expose evaluation limits. In controlled experiments, Farrow et al. [17] show that clinical h<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>cr</sub>it<sub>er</sub>i<sub>a</sub> <sub>can</sub> l<sub>eave</sub> <sub>ru</sub>b<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>scores</sub> <sub>unc</sub>h<sub>ange</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s.</sub> Process supervision provides intermediate-step feedback[18], while trajectory synthesis and selection address rationale quality, dificulty, and distribution[48, 49]. ## F.2 Learning from Failed Trajectories and Localized Repair

Fai<sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> trajectories contain reusa<sup>bl</sup>e reasonin as we<sup>ll</sup> as errors. Se<sup>lf</sup>-Re<sup>fi</sup>ne an<sup>d</sup> Re<sup>fl</sup>exion im rove su<sup>b</sup>se uent attempts through self-feedback and verbal memory without weight updates [53, 54]. Learning from Mistakes (LEMA) constructs mistake–correction pairs, TrajFusion combines incorrect and correct trajectories, and Exploring Ex<sub>p</sub>ert Failures (EEF) extracts useful actions from failed demonstrations [6, 10, 55]. Hindsi<sub>g</sub>ht Hint Distillation (HHD) and FailFor<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>uide recover<sub>y</sub> with concise hints or skills instead of full teacher solutions [1, 56].

Local recovery also depends on where guidance enters and how it becomes supervision. Error-localization studies, backtracking-based distillation, Test-Time Scaling via Error Localization (TTEL), and Selective Regenerative Decodin<sub>g</sub> investi<sub>g</sub>ate localization, backtrackin<sub>g</sub> corrections, and <sub>p</sub>refix reuse [5, 21, 22, 57]. Self-Correction via Reinforcement Learning (SCoRe) learns self-correction through reinforcement learning; Project Aletheia and P<sub>y</sub>li<sub>gen</sub>t d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> f<sub>rom ver</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>ac</sub>kt<sub>rac</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>races or searc</sub>h t<sub>rees; m</sub>i<sub>cro-re</sub>fl<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve se</sub>lf<sub>-</sub>di<sub>s</sub>till<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> combines micro-reflective trajectories with ca<sub>p</sub>abilit<sub>y</sub>-de<sub>p</sub>endent selection [24, 58, 59, 60]. EvoThink and Reflective On-Polic<sub>y</sub> Self-Distillation (ROSD) instead use <sub>p</sub>reference learnin<sub>g</sub> and reflective on-<sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> self-distillation [61, 62]. MedEvo uses medically grounded, same-student local navigation: preserving supported prefixes, guiding <sub>co</sub>ntin<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>go</sub>ld<sub>-</sub>blind <sub>au</sub>ditin<sub>g</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd t<sub>ag-co</sub>nditi<sub>o</sub>n<sub>e</sub>d hint<sub>s,</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>rn<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>supe</sub>r<sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n<sub>.</sub>

## F.3 Data Evolution and Recursive Self-Improvement

STaR and ReST<sup>EM</sup> select correct self-generated reasoning for supervision[7, 8]. EvoThink and ROSD use preference learning and reflective self-distillation[61, 62]; capability-dependent selection matches supervision and students [24]. Recursive self-improvement examines how feedback changes subsequent learning experience [63]. In clinical interaction, MediSkill-Evo [25] evolves validated clinical, process, symbolic, and visual knowledge banks without fine-tuning the backbone, using process constraints to guide evidence-grounded actions. MedEvo instead updates mo<sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup> weig<sup>h</sup>ts t<sup>h</sup>roug<sup>h</sup> SFT on repaire<sup>d</sup> stu<sup>d</sup>ent trajectories: it <sup>d</sup>iagnoses me<sup>d</sup>ica<sup>l</sup> groups an<sup>d</sup> error patterns on a fixed feedback set, then generates and repairs training-pool trajectories for subsequent self-distillation.

## F.4 Failure Feedback beyond Medical Reasoning

Failure-aware a<sub>pp</sub>roaches also or<sub>g</sub>anize recover<sub>y</sub> usin<sub>g</sub> structured failure feedback in a<sub>g</sub>ent safet<sub>y</sub> and robotics [64, 65, 66].

## G.2 Trajectory-Supervision Baselines

Baseline 1: HHD. Hindsight Hint Distillation uses concise hints to scafold recovery [1]. Its role in the comparison is to assess hint-based supervision alongside MedEvo’s medical diagnosis, prefix preservation, and feedback-driven data updates. The method follows the cited work; Tab. 2 re orts our medical-benchmark evaluation.

Baseline 2: TrajFusion. TrajFusion combines incorrect and correct trajectories to construct training supervision [10]. It provides a comparison for learning from failed reasoning. MedEvo instead constructs a corrected sufix from the <sub>same s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t’<sub>s re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x an</sub>d <sub>uses me</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>l f<sub>ee</sub>db<sub>ac</sub>k t<sub>o pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub>iti<sub>ze</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>er repa</sub>i<sub>rs.</sub>

Baseline 3: Teacher SFT. This baseline uses SFT on supervision generated by Qwen-3.8-Max. It compares teacher-generated supervision with MedEvo’s repaired student trajectories. This isolates supervision.
